id="sidebarContent"><p class="sidebar_navTopLabel__KLlOw">Contents</p><ol><li><a class="sidebar_link__NRCVd sidebar_linkActive__gwV6j" rel="noopener" href="#article">Article</a></li><li><a class="sidebar_link__NRCVd" rel="noopener" href="#more-blogs">More blogs</a></li></ol></div><div class="sidebar_copyUrlWrapper__AaTG4  hidden lg:flex" style="border-bottom:none"><button type="button" class="sidebar_copyUrl__cRt0X">Copy URL</button></div><div class="sidebar_copyUrlWrapper__AaTG4 flex lg:hidden" style="border-bottom:none;border-top:none"><button type="button" class="sidebar_copyUrl__cRt0X">Copy URL</button></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="hidden lg:block" style="margin-bottom:-0px"></div><div class="" id="article"><div class=""><div class="bg-white "><div class="rebrand-container py-0"><div class="default-grid"><div class="col-span-full lg:col-start-4 wysiwyg_module_root__TObB_"><div class="wysiwyg_module_content__8emWC"><p>Recently, the Zscaler ThreatLabZ team came across new information-stealer malware called Immortal, which is written in .NET and designed to steal sensitive information from an infected machine. The Immortal stealer is sold on the dark web with different build-based subscriptions. This blog provides an analysis of the data Immortal steals from browsers, the files it steals (and the applications it steals from), and what it does with the stolen data.</p>

<p>Immortal starts its infection by creating a directory with a random name in a temp folder. Next, it creates a <em>password.log</em> file in &quot;\%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\password.log”.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/password_log-edited.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p>Immortal writes the malware name, author’s name, and telegram address of the author in a <em>password.log</em> file.</p>

<ul>
	<li>Date: Current date and time  “MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss”</li>
	<li>Windows Username: Username</li>
	<li>HWID: MachineGuid</li>
	<li>System: Operating system name</li>
</ul>

<h3><strong>Browser info stealing</strong></h3>

<p>Immortal steals data from 24 browsers. It steals stored credentials, cookies, credit card data, and autofill data from the targeted browsers.</p>

<p>When the user saves a username and password in the targeted browser, it stores the data in a “Login Data” file in an SQLite database format, and the browser-stored cookie information in the “Cookies” file. It also stores autofill data, credit card data, and other web information in the “Web Data” file. Below are the file paths for those files:</p>

<ul>
	<li>“\%AppData%\Local\{Browser}\User Data\Default\Login Data”</li>
	<li>“\%AppData%\Local\{Browser}\User Data\Default\Web Data”</li>
	<li>“\%AppData%\Local\{Browser}\User Data\Default\Cookies”</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>List of targeted browsers:</strong></p>

<ul>
	<li>Chrome</li>
	<li>Yandex</li>
	<li>Orbitum</li>
	<li>Opera</li>
	<li>Amigo</li>
	<li>CentBrowser</li>
	<li>Torch</li>
	<li>Comodo</li>
	<li>Go!</li>
	<li>ChromePlus</li>
	<li>Uran</li>
	<li>BlackHawk</li>
	<li>CoolNovo</li>
	<li>AcWebBrowser</li>
	<li>Epic Browser</li>
	<li>Baidu Spark</li>
	<li>Rockmelt</li>
	<li>Sleipnir</li>
	<li>SRWare Iron</li>
	<li>Titan Browser</li>
	<li>Flock</li>
	<li>Vivaldi</li>
	<li>Sputnik</li>
	<li>Maxthon</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Credential stealing</strong></p>

<p>The malware fetches credentials from the “Login Data” file and stores them in the <em>password.log</em> file as per the format below: Path: ” \%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\password.log”.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/browser_steal_format.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<ul>
	<li>SiteUrl: Website URL</li>
	<li>Login: Username</li>
	<li>Password: Password</li>
	<li>Program: Targeted browser</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Cookie stealing</strong></p>

<p>Immortal fetches cookie data from the cookies file and stores it in <em>{Browsername}_cookies.txt file</em>.</p>

<p>Path: “\%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\Cookies\{Browsername_cookies.txt}&quot;. The format is shown below.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Credit%20card%20code%20edited.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>Credit card data</strong></p>

<p>Immortal fetches credit card data from the “Web Data” file and stores it in the <em>{Browsername</em><em>}_CC.txt</em> file.</p>

<p>Path: “\%AppData%\{Random_DirName}\CC\{Browsername_CC.txt}”. The format is shown below.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Credit%20card%20code%20edited.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>Autofill data</strong></p>

<p>The autofill feature of a browser allows the user to store commonly entered information in web forms. This information might include username, email, password, address, and credit card information. So, when the user opens a web page, it will automatically fill in the information already saved by the browser. The autofill information is stored in the “Web Data” file.</p>

<p>Immortal fetches autofill data from the “Web Data” file and stores it in the <em>{Autofill}_CC.txt</em> file.</p>

<p>Path: “\%AppData%\{Random_DirName}\Autofill\{Browsername_Autofill.txt}”. The format is shown below.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Autofill%20code%20edited.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<h3> </h3>

<h3><strong>File stealing</strong></h3>

<p>Immortal steals files from many different applications. The details are below.</p>

<p><strong>Minecraft launchers</strong></p>

<p>The malware steals user data files and sessions from Minecraft launcher applications. The malware copies those applications&#x27; files into “%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\Applications\{AppName}\”. The following is a list of the applications:</p>

<ul>
	<li>MinecraftOnly</li>
	<li>McSkill</li>
	<li>LavaCraft</li>
	<li>MinecraftLauncher</li>
	<li>VimeWorld</li>
	<li>RedServer</li>
</ul>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Minecraft%20app%20code.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>Steam</strong></p>

<p>The malware steals files for the Steam application. Steam is an application for playing, discussing, and creating games. The files stolen by Immortal are as follows:</p>

<ul>
	<li>SSFN (2 files)</li>
	<li>VDF files from the config folder
	<ul>
		<li>Config.vdf</li>
		<li>loginusers.vdf</li>
	</ul>
	</li>
</ul>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Steam%20files%20stealing%20code.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>Telegram and Discord</strong></p>

<p>Immortal also steals session-related files from Telegram and Discord. Telegram is a cloud-based instant messaging and voice over IP service. Discord is the cross-platform voice and text chat application designed to help gamers talk to each other in real time. Immortal copies those files into “%Temp%\{Random_Name}\Applications\{AppName}\”.</p>

<p>File Path:</p>

<ul>
	<li>%AppData%\Telegram Desktop\tdata\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\</li>
	<li>%AppData%\Telegram Desktop\tdata\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\map0</li>
	<li>%AppData%\Telegram Desktop\tdata\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\map1</li>
	<li>%AppData%\discord\\Local Storage\\https_discordapp.com_0.localstorage</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>FileZilla</strong></p>

<p>Immortal steals files that contain FileZilla credentials. FileZilla is a known FTP tool used for file transfer. The malware copies the below files into “\%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\FileZilla\”.</p>

<ul>
	<li>\%AppData%\Filezilla\recentservers.xml</li>
	<li>\%AppData%\Filezilla\sitemanager.xml</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Bitcoin-Qt wallet</strong></p>

<p>Immortal steals <em>wallet.dat</em> files from Bitcoin-Qt, a free and open-source Bitcoin wallet software. Below is a screenshot of the code for fetching the wallet path from the registry. The malware copies the <em>wallet.dat</em> file in “%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\”.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_bitcoin-edited.png" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>Desktop files</strong></p>

<p>Immortal also goes through every file in the desktop folder on the victim’s system. It steals extension files (listed below) and copies them into “%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\Files\”.</p>

<ul>
	<li>Txt</li>
	<li>Log</li>
	<li>Doc</li>
	<li>Docx</li>
	<li>sql</li>
</ul>

<p><strong>Screenshot &amp; Webcam</strong></p>

<p>Immortal takes a screenshot of the desktop of the infected system and saves it in “\%AppData%\{Random_DirName}\desktop.jpg”. It also captures a webcam snapshot and saves in it “\%AppData%\{Random_DirName}\CamPicture.jpg”.<br/>
 </p>

<h3><strong>Network communication</strong></h3>

<p>The malware stores all the stolen data in the directory “\%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\”. After that, it compresses all the files in a ZIP archive and saves the compressed file in <em>\%Temp%\{Random_filename}.zip</em>. Further, it sends <em>{Random_filename}.zip</em> to its command-and-control server as shown below. It also deletes the “\%Temp%\{Random_DirName}\” before sending the ZIP file.</p>

<p><img alt="Image" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_senddata.png" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<ul>
	<li>User = User name</li>
	<li>Hwid = MachineGuid</li>
</ul>

<p>At the time of analysis, the command &amp; control panel for this stealer was live.</p>

<p><img alt="image 1" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_panel2e%CC%84dited_0.png" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p>We found the Immortal stealer being advertised and sold with different build-based subscriptions. The following is a screenshot of a page that describes all of Immortal&#x27;s functionality and cost per build. A per-post price for one build is $30.</p>

<p><img alt="Dark web market" src="https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Dark%20web%20market%20e%CC%84dited.PNG" loading="lazy" decoding="async"/></p>

<p><strong>IOCs</strong></p>

<p>Md5: 1719ff4ff267ef598a1dcee1d5b68667</p>

<p>Downloading URL : www.appleidservice[.]jp/stealer/files/svhost.exe</p>

<p>NetworkURL: www.appleidservice[.]jp/stealer/files/upload.php</p>

<p> </p>

the Zscaler ThreatLabZ team came across new information-stealer malware called Immortal, which is written in .NET and designed to steal sensitive information from an infected machine. The Immortal stealer is sold on the dark web with different build-based subscriptions. This blog provides an analysis of the data Immortal steals from browsers, the files it steals (and the applications it steals from), and what it does with the stolen data.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal starts its infection by creating a directory with a random name in a temp folder. Next, it creates a \u003cem\u003epassword.log\u003c/em\u003e file in \"\\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\password.log”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/password_log-edited.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal writes the malware name, author’s name, and telegram address of the author in a \u003cem\u003epassword.log\u003c/em\u003e file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eDate: Current date and time  “MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss”\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eWindows Username: Username\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eHWID: MachineGuid\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSystem: Operating system name\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBrowser info stealing\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal steals data from 24 browsers. It steals stored credentials, cookies, credit card data, and autofill data from the targeted browsers.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eWhen the user saves a username and password in the targeted browser, it stores the data in a “Login Data” file in an SQLite database format, and the browser-stored cookie information in the “Cookies” file. It also stores autofill data, credit card data, and other web information in the “Web Data” file. Below are the file paths for those files:\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e“\\%AppData%\\Local\\{Browser}\\User Data\\Default\\Login Data”\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e“\\%AppData%\\Local\\{Browser}\\User Data\\Default\\Web Data”\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e“\\%AppData%\\Local\\{Browser}\\User Data\\Default\\Cookies”\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eList of targeted browsers:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eChrome\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eYandex\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eOrbitum\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eOpera\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eAmigo\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eCentBrowser\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eTorch\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eComodo\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eGo!\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eChromePlus\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eUran\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eBlackHawk\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eCoolNovo\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eAcWebBrowser\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eEpic Browser\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eBaidu Spark\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eRockmelt\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSleipnir\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSRWare Iron\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eTitan Browser\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eFlock\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eVivaldi\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSputnik\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eMaxthon\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCredential stealing\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eThe malware fetches credentials from the “Login Data” file and stores them in the \u003cem\u003epassword.log\u003c/em\u003e file as per the format below: Path: ” \\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\password.log”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/browser_steal_format.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSiteUrl: Website URL\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eLogin: Username\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003ePassword: Password\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eProgram: Targeted browser\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCookie stealing\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal fetches cookie data from the cookies file and stores it in \u003cem\u003e{Browsername}_cookies.txt file\u003c/em\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003ePath: “\\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\Cookies\\{Browsername_cookies.txt}\". The format is shown below.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Credit%20card%20code%20edited.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCredit card data\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal fetches credit card data from the “Web Data” file and stores it in the \u003cem\u003e{Browsername\u003c/em\u003e\u003cem\u003e}_CC.txt\u003c/em\u003e file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003ePath: “\\%AppData%\\{Random_DirName}\\CC\\{Browsername_CC.txt}”. The format is shown below.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Credit%20card%20code%20edited.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAutofill data\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eThe autofill feature of a browser allows the user to store commonly entered information in web forms. This information might include username, email, password, address, and credit card information. So, when the user opens a web page, it will automatically fill in the information already saved by the browser. The autofill information is stored in the “Web Data” file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal fetches autofill data from the “Web Data” file and stores it in the \u003cem\u003e{Autofill}_CC.txt\u003c/em\u003e file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003ePath: “\\%AppData%\\{Random_DirName}\\Autofill\\{Browsername_Autofill.txt}”. The format is shown below.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Autofill%20code%20edited.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003ch3\u003e \u003c/h3\u003e\n\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFile stealing\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal steals files from many different applications. The details are below.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMinecraft launchers\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eThe malware steals user data files and sessions from Minecraft launcher applications. The malware copies those applications' files into “%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\Applications\\{AppName}\\”. The following is a list of the applications:\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eMinecraftOnly\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eMcSkill\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eLavaCraft\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eMinecraftLauncher\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eVimeWorld\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eRedServer\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Minecraft%20app%20code.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSteam\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eThe malware steals files for the Steam application. Steam is an application for playing, discussing, and creating games. The files stolen by Immortal are as follows:\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eSSFN (2 files)\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eVDF files from the config folder\n\t\u003cul\u003e\n\t\t\u003cli\u003eConfig.vdf\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\t\u003cli\u003eloginusers.vdf\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003c/ul\u003e\n\t\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/Steam%20files%20stealing%20code.PNG\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTelegram and Discord\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal also steals session-related files from Telegram and Discord. Telegram is a cloud-based instant messaging and voice over IP service. Discord is the cross-platform voice and text chat application designed to help gamers talk to each other in real time. Immortal copies those files into “%Temp%\\{Random_Name}\\Applications\\{AppName}\\”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eFile Path:\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e%AppData%\\Telegram Desktop\\tdata\\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\\\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e%AppData%\\Telegram Desktop\\tdata\\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\\map0\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e%AppData%\\Telegram Desktop\\tdata\\D877F783D5D3EF8C1\\map1\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e%AppData%\\discord\\\\Local Storage\\\\https_discordapp.com_0.localstorage\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFileZilla\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal steals files that contain FileZilla credentials. FileZilla is a known FTP tool used for file transfer. The malware copies the below files into “\\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\FileZilla\\”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e\\%AppData%\\Filezilla\\recentservers.xml\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003e\\%AppData%\\Filezilla\\sitemanager.xml\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBitcoin-Qt wallet\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal steals \u003cem\u003ewallet.dat\u003c/em\u003e files from Bitcoin-Qt, a free and open-source Bitcoin wallet software. Below is a screenshot of the code for fetching the wallet path from the registry. The malware copies the \u003cem\u003ewallet.dat\u003c/em\u003e file in “%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_bitcoin-edited.png\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDesktop files\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal also goes through every file in the desktop folder on the victim’s system. It steals extension files (listed below) and copies them into “%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\Files\\”.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eTxt\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eLog\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eDoc\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eDocx\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003esql\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eScreenshot \u0026amp; Webcam\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eImmortal takes a screenshot of the desktop of the infected system and saves it in “\\%AppData%\\{Random_DirName}\\desktop.jpg”. It also captures a webcam snapshot and saves in it “\\%AppData%\\{Random_DirName}\\CamPicture.jpg”.\u003cbr\u003e\n \u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003ch3\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNetwork communication\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/h3\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eThe malware stores all the stolen data in the directory “\\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\”. After that, it compresses all the files in a ZIP archive and saves the compressed file in \u003cem\u003e\\%Temp%\\{Random_filename}.zip\u003c/em\u003e. Further, it sends \u003cem\u003e{Random_filename}.zip\u003c/em\u003e to its command-and-control server as shown below. It also deletes the “\\%Temp%\\{Random_DirName}\\” before sending the ZIP file.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"Image\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_senddata.png\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cul\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eUser = User name\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\u003cli\u003eHwid = MachineGuid\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eAt the time of analysis, the command \u0026amp; control panel for this stealer was live.\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"image 1\" src=\"https://cms.zscaler.com/cdn-cgi/image/format=auto/sites/default/files/images/blogs/Immortal_stealer/immortal_panel2e%CC%84dited_0.png\" loading=\"lazy\" decoding=\"async\"\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\n\u003cp\u003eWe found the Immortal stealer being advertised and sold with different build-based subscriptions. The following is a screenshot of a page that describes all of Immortal's functionality and cost per build.
